# Who she is — reader 02

Read on 2026-11-17 for the agent merging the three reader shares. This is a reader evidence digest for question 1, not the final avatar or the short findings page.

All 992 assigned records were read, including quote, context, speaker, stage, themes and source. The four files contain 235, 299, 211 and 247 records respectively. There are 366 source post ids. These are selected Reddit posts and original-poster replies collected for question 2, not a representative sample of mothers or buyers. All counts below count quote records, never people, buyers or prevalence.

One post can supply many records; crossposts, longer and shorter versions and later updates also recur. Eleven groups have exactly repeated quote text within this share. Counts preserve distinct record ids rather than treating repeated records as independent testimony. Patterns overlap, so their counts must not be added.

What is counted: the listed supporting records, read with their stored context and, where opened, their own source post. A short excerpt can carry relevant context without stating every detail itself. “Source-only” notes identify additions from the opened post: those words are paraphrased, never passed off as a stored quotation. Counts describe the evidence attached to each pattern, not every utterance in the full Reddit threads.

Every displayed quotation is the complete stored quote field, including its existing omissions, spelling, punctuation and Markdown. A quotation is displayed once; later patterns refer to its id. A record can support one point and limit a broader interpretation of another. Counterexamples/limits below test a uniform portrait, not the truth of an individual disclosure.

Parent_unspecified remains an unspecified parent unless the person identifies herself in the source. Schema labels are not proof of gender, first-child status, occupation or diagnosis. Mothers’ direct statements, other caregivers’ accounts and commercial material are labelled separately.

## What the excerpts do not tell us

This audit uses the quote plus context of each record, not facts propagated from all other excerpts of the same post. It therefore has a deliberately narrower denominator than the source-expanded patterns below.

| Detail | Records explicitly giving a sign in quote/context | Records giving no sign there |

|---|---:|---:|

| Mother’s own stated age | 3 | 989 |

| Poster’s own paid work, former work, leave or stay-at-home role | 92 | 900 |

| A numeric personal or household income amount | 0 | 992 |

The three age-bearing record/context pairs are dv05-0174, dv05-0184, dv21-0011. Baby ages, weeks pregnant, a grandmother’s age, and another adult’s age do not identify maternal age. Additional ages found only in the opened source posts are set out under her situation.

The work-sign coding includes former professional experience as a sign, but does not turn “my husband works”, being home for a day, nursery use, or membership of r/sahm/r/workingmoms into the poster’s own employment status. Records coded as having a sign: dv04-0003, dv04-0004, dv04-0035, dv04-0036, dv04-0037, dv04-0040, dv04-0057, dv04-0068, dv04-0111, dv04-0122, dv04-0145, dv04-0149, dv04-0176, dv04-0185, dv04-0193, dv04-0194, dv04-0213, dv04-0216, dv04-0221, dv04-0230, dv05-0039, dv05-0072, dv05-0080, dv05-0085, dv05-0096, dv05-0097, dv05-0105, dv05-0125, dv05-0146, dv05-0147, dv05-0148, dv05-0149, dv05-0151, dv05-0153, dv05-0158, dv05-0165, dv05-0174, dv05-0182, dv05-0197, dv05-0205, dv05-0211, dv05-0217, dv05-0223, dv05-0228, dv05-0230, dv05-0231, dv05-0234, dv05-0235, dv05-0236, dv05-0237, dv05-0240, dv05-0258, dv05-0260, dv05-0287, dv05-0289, dv05-0298, dv06-0036, dv06-0049, dv06-0065, dv06-0066, dv06-0077, dv06-0082, dv06-0094, dv06-0099, dv06-0128, dv06-0130, dv06-0133, dv06-0144, dv06-0156, dv06-0169, dv06-0173, dv06-0176, dv06-0179, dv06-0180, dv21-0002, dv21-0021, dv21-0057, dv21-0063, dv21-0065, dv21-0080, dv21-0081, dv21-0098, dv21-0100, dv21-0102, dv21-0113, dv21-0154, dv21-0155, dv21-0170, dv21-0174, dv21-0228, dv21-0230, dv21-0240. The remaining 900 records cannot establish an employment profile from their quote/context alone.

The absence of an income amount is not evidence of either poverty or affluence. Specific budgets, prices, inability to afford care and statements that money is not the issue are retained below.

For orientation only, stored speaker labels are 384 new_mother, 66 experienced_mother, 491 parent_unspecified, 46 partner and 5 relative_caregiver. The label partner includes at least one self-identified first-time mother with a wife. There are no app-store/book reviewer records in this share.

Stored stages: newborn_0_8_weeks 35; baby_2_4_months 129; baby_4_6_months 125; baby_6_9_months 224; baby_9_12_months 149; toddler_12_18_months 170; toddler_18_24_months 106; looking_back 21; unknown 33. These are baby-stage labels, not maternal ages.

## Her situation

### 1. First babies and little prior reference

Some posters explicitly say this is their first baby, or that they have little experience of babies; reassurance helps them decide what is normal.

Count: 173 supporting quote records in this share.

First-time status comes from an explicit statement or the record context, not the new_mother label. Some overlapping records below come from the same first-time parent.

### 2. Older children, stepchildren, twins and another pregnancy

Others already have older children, twins or another pregnancy; time and attention must be divided.

Count: 89 supporting quote records in this share.

Source clarification: the mother in dv04-0027 stopped paid work two weeks earlier and was nearly 38 weeks pregnant. The dv04-0233 source has a two-year-old, ten-month-old twins and a fourth pregnancy. Do not describe all mothers as first-time mothers.

### 3. Her own age is rarely disclosed in the record excerpts

Only three quote/context pairs explicitly state the mother's age; additional ages are visible in several of the opened source posts.

Count: 3 supporting quote records in this share.

The record contexts give 21 for dv05-0174 and 20 for dv05-0184; dv21-0011 says mid twenties. Opened-source additions: dv04-0045 says 25F; dv05-0083 says 31F; dv06-0008 says 28; dv06-0140 says both parents are in their mid 30s; dv21-0174 says 28, diagnosed with OCD since 17. These values do not establish a typical buyer age. The stay-at-home father source behind dv21-0154 separately says early thirties; it is not maternal-age evidence.

[dv05-0174]
> I can’t get anything done while he is awake, I can’t move without him screaming and chasing after me, no toilet, can’t get ready for work, can’t eat during the day.

[dv05-0184]
> I feel so guilty for letting my son do independent play time, I feel like he gets way too much and I try to play with him and he just doesn’t seem interested.

[dv21-0011]
> I am in my mid twenties, and have an 11 month old daughter. She is very curious & active. She loves to be outside, but I’m finding it difficult lately, and feel extremely guilty when she is bored and I don’t have it in me to go outside.

Further supporting ids: none beyond the records above.

Counterexamples: no record in this share contradicts the individual disclosure. This is a small, explicitly identified circumstance or speaker group; it must not be generalised to the rest.

### 4. Work has restarted or is about to restart

Return to paid work happens at different baby ages, and leave deadlines can force the childcare decision.

Count: 44 supporting quote records in this share.

Source-only details: dv04-0061 has finished maternity leave in May; partner's shared leave ends in September, then grandmothers cover until nursery at one. dv04-0185 returns in January. dv05-0258 has two more months off after a partner's 12 weeks. dv05-0289 has four months left. dv06-0082 returned at three months; dv06-0088 expects roughly four months; dv06-0179 returned at four months. dv21-0080 returns in August. These are individual accounts, not country leave rules.

### 5. Paid work competes with care, and keeping work can matter to her

Working parents describe exhaustion, scarce time together and missed first milestones; one nurse explicitly wants her work and adult interaction.

Count: 51 supporting quote records in this share.

Source-only detail: dv05-0234 works two jobs, one for health insurance because her husband's job has none. dv04-0193 runs a business for two to three hours a day, including weekends, with a partner who also works at home. dv05-0260 works full time, has a nanny and a flexible partner, but handles breastfeeding nights alone. Work is not always a financial preference or always unwanted.

### 14. Prematurity, hospital stays and infant illness change the starting point

Some families describe prematurity, NICU time, reflux or other conditions that restrict play, outings, feeding or the age used to judge progress.

### 16. Minutes to eat, wash or use the toilet are a meaningful win

Many want five to twenty minutes for food, a coffee, washing, the toilet or a single chore rather than a long leisure block.

### 17. Naps can immobilise her instead of freeing time

### 18. Exhaustion changes how much she can speak, smile and think

[dv21-0241]
> Forth, break your routine! This can simply be taking your play mat outside and having a play in the garden. I can bet your LO will be more relaxed purely from the change of scenery and so will you. Or simply just do something different! Pop them in their high chair and give them a bowl of water and some toys (supervised of course), if they enjoy baths just throw a random bath in there. If you’re feeling brave take them to the swimming baths. Pop them in the car and go shopping (not groceries, go get something you want - treat yourself!) I was always hesitant to do this because I became reliant on being close to the things my baby needed (bottles, food etc.) but I put my big girl pants on, bought the things I needed to leave the house and let me tell you, the days went so quickly and I actually enjoyed myself!

[dv21-0242]
> Finally, create a safe space for your baby where you can put them to have a few minutes to yourself when things get hard. If they’re super young, a cot is just fine. As long as they’re fed and changed there is no shame in stepping away for a few brief moments to get your composure. If they’re on the crawl and pull up like mine is - get a small play pen with some toys. Take a few moments to yourself. I was always hesitant to do this, I believed my baby crying would be detrimental to them. But in reality, 5 minutes of crying while you step away isn’t going to harm them but not taking them 5 minutes for yourself will take its toll on you over time.

### 20. Outings and classes can give the day structure

What's kept me going is the belief that at 1 year old he will be so much better but with it approaching very soon ive never felt more lost/depressed. Idk if im even looking for advice at this point just maybe someone who had such a hard baby as me just letting me know it gets better!

## Who else has a say

### 24. Partners can prescribe standards without carrying the same day

### 27. Daycare both observes development and supplies care or stimulation

Nursery staff and assessments can introduce a worry, threaten placement or reassure through photos, progress and ordinary participation.

A parent's fear of being dismissed from daycare is different from the actual dismissal in dv21-0064. The quoted daycare screen-policy wording in dv05-0095 says 'minimum'; preserve it as reported rather than silently correcting it.

### 28. Friends, cousins, groups and strangers become comparison points

Other babies and other adults' comments are a yardstick, and public groups can leave a mother conspicuous, judged or tearful.

Count: 149 supporting quote records in this share.

Comparison may be to a sibling or a cousin in the same house, not just to an influencer. dv21-0040 explicitly says she struggled at school. dv21-0159 says she and her relatives were gifted/early-talking; neither gives an education level for the wider group.

### 29. People online can offer solidarity, usable ideas or another layer of judgement

Posters seek reassurance and practical experience, change purchases or plans after replies, and sometimes feel dismissed or defensive.

Count: 326 supporting quote records in this share.

General advice is not evidence that the poster followed it. Decisions ('I will buy') are distinguished from completed purchases. Counterexamples here are hostile responses that make future help-seeking less likely, not lack of internet use.

## What she already follows and believes

### 30. Apps and packaged guides can make 'should' feel like a daily obligation

Parents name tracking and subscription tools, and some feel their baby's timing or toy use must match notifications and instructions.

Count: 36 supporting quote records in this share.

Names include Lovevery, Wonder Weeks and Huckleberry. dv05-0045 wants a fun milestone log and calls herself not very anxious; do not portray tracking as uniformly harmful. No paid app purchase is assumed simply because an app is used.

[dv04-0018]
> Wake windows, developmental leaps, growth spurts, sleep pressure, overtired vs undertired, sleep associations, feeding schedules, tummy time, milestones, independent sleep, nap lengths, reflux..the list never ends.

[dv04-0019]
> If she doesn’t nap when the app says she should, I worry. If she wants another feed sooner than expected, I worry. It feels like there’s a “right” way to do everything, and I’m scared I’m getting it wrong.

[dv04-0020]
> Did you eventually stop tracking everything and just start following your baby’s cues?

[dv05-0013]
> the content in the playkits and on the website is overwhelming and borderline stressful. I feel like I have information overload, and my day revolves around all the items I should be using to make sure his development progresses properly.

[dv05-0014]
> If I go a day without black and white flashcards or we just didn’t get around to tummy time, I feel really guilty and like I’m wronging/neglecting him.

[dv05-0015]
> Or if they send a notification saying “baby might be hitting this milestone!”, and he isn’t, I’m worried that I haven’t done enough and it’s my fault he’s ‘behind’.

[dv05-0016]
> Lately it’s been making me take pause and wonder if dropping the subscription would be better for my mental health, which is so backwards compared to their supposed mission as a company.

[dv05-0017]
> Yep I was introduced to wonder weeks a while back and it easily creates the same anxious feelings if I focus on it too long.

[dv05-0019]
> but then they go a little over the top with the number of items, and the style of some, even if they’re still beneficial. Like why do I have several items for texture exploration, when one would be enough.

[dv05-0020]
> like another user said it takes the thought out of buying toys when they just show up

[dv05-0037]
> I wanted to ask if the Montessori kind of subscription toys worth starting at this age? I looked up some products at Amazon too, which has high contrast cards, mirrors and sensory toys for kids to play with during tummy time but these subscription based models have everything in one place. I been getting ads for Lovevery, Kiwico and some others. I do like the concept of included parents guide.

[dv05-0044]
> I already use huckleberry, but is there any other apps that you guys find useful? Specifically looking for an app that maybe tracks babies development milestones but open to any you suggest!

[dv05-0251]
> We subscribe to Lovevery and my kid doesn’t… engage with the toys the way he “should.” He just throws them (or tries to put them in his mouth).

[dv05-0252]
> Should I be concerned that he’s not hitting some of these cognitive, Montessori milestones?

[dv05-0255]
> Also gives me good perspective: maybe these toys aren’t his kind of toy.

[dv06-0106]
> It was different videos about toddler playing with magna tiles or blocks or this new "trend" with rainbow stacker and faceless dolls?

[dv06-0107]
> I tried to do a cute sort sorting activity with him... lasted 2 second before he walked off🤦🏻‍♀️😅

### 31. Montessori, independent play and child-led exploration are stated ideals

Some want open-ended toys, accessible shelves, a 'yes' space and space for the child to explore; they also ask for flexible structure.

Count: 102 supporting quote records in this share.

Unruffled is the one explicitly named podcast in this share. Montessori is sometimes a purchase label or a pressure source rather than a coherent method the parent says she follows. 'Yes space' is the poster's term.

[dv04-0014]
> But I don’t really spend much time at all “playing with her” like with her toys or doing a play activity. I feel guilty as if I’m not spending enough time with her but I also find it so adorable when she plays independently and I don’t want to interrupt it.

[dv04-0015]
> I feel almost ruthless not playing with her that much sometimes when she’s asking me to, but I really feel that she should learn to explore toys more independently

[dv04-0016]
> I’ve listened to a lot of Unruffled podcasts about how independent play is so important. But I don’t want my daughter to think I’m just always ignoring her - I DO play with her a ton but don’t want to play all day.

> Also adding that we do toy swaps etc to keep his toys “interesting” and also not having all his toys out at once so it’s overwhelming.

[dv04-0102]

[dv05-0002]

### 32. Constant interaction is treated as proof of being a good parent

Parents equate doing enough with talking, teaching, practising and keeping the baby engaged; even peaceful independent play can feel like neglect.

Count: 481 supporting quote records in this share.

This pattern records her rules and self-judgement. It does not establish that delayed milestones were caused by too little interaction, paid work, depression or independent play.

### 33. Screens have age rules, content rules and device rules

Posters distinguish TV from phones/tablets, slower shows from rapid content, background use from active watching and short sessions from all-day use.

Count: 157 supporting quote records in this share.

Named content includes Ms Rachel, Mimi Soleil, Ms Apple, Teletubbies, Baby Einstein, Tumble Leaf, Studio Ghibli, TWD, Winnie the Pooh, Coraline, Bluey, Daniel Tiger, Sesame Street, Mr Rogers, Cocomelon, Masha and the Bear, Blippi, Nat Geo, Reading Rainbow, Hungry Caterpillar and Timothy Goes to School. These names identify habits, not recommendations. Reported NHS/pediatrician cutoffs are beliefs/advice received, not independently verified rules.

### 34. A developmental explanation can become a framework for the whole day

Parents use wake windows, leaps, regressions, corrected age, milestone cutoffs and screening questions to interpret behaviour and decide what to do.

Count: 344 supporting quote records in this share.

Some ask how much crying to permit for learning; others prioritise stopping distress. Corrected age is raised as a question, not a reader calculation. A parent's interpretation of a screening tool or condition stays labelled as their interpretation.

### 36. Cheap objects, DIY play and second-hand shopping are real alternatives

Parents use household objects, toy rotation and simple activities, or look for used equipment rather than assuming another premium kit is needed.

Count: 60 supporting quote records in this share.

Examples include masking tape, a heavy box, empty bottles, keys, cardboard, crayons, a salad spinner, parchment paper, leaflets and water. A failed household-object activity is a counterexample to assuming DIY always works. Looking on Marketplace is a search, not a completed purchase.

### 38. They have already practised physical skills in many ways

Families try tummy time, positioning, supported standing, floor play, demonstrations and therapist exercises, often repeatedly despite distress.

Speech-related concern often coexists with daily books, narration, signs, songs or flashcards, rather than no attempted interaction.

Count: 272 supporting quote records in this share.

Skill acquisition and lack of response can be emotionally significant because she wants reciprocal communication, not merely a checked milestone. No causal conclusion about the benefit of an exercise follows from these anecdotes.

### 40. Therapy and assessments are already sought, used, doubted or blocked

Parents self-refer, attend public or private therapy, wait for services, seek another provider or ask whether treatment is delivering enough.

Count: 212 supporting quote records in this share.

Not all therapy is purchased: EI/program involvement without stated payment is service use. Specific costs, waits, eligibility refusals and provider complaints stay in the account rather than being reduced to general 'needs reassurance'.

> My son is 18 months, doesn't crawl, walk, stand nor is he able to go from laying down to sitting up. He just started PT & OT. He tends to look into space and even if im standing in front of him its hard to get his attention. I try to convince myself that there's some logical explanation as to why he does the things that he does. Im scared. Im disappointed. Im not sure what I did wrong. ## How she sees herself

### 42. She can judge herself by the baby's milestones even while saying babies differ

Posters call themselves failing, inadequate or bad mothers and blame work, missed practice, toys or their own choices despite acknowledging individual variation.

Count: 515 supporting quote records in this share.

The repeated self-blame is a description of her self-image, not evidence that she caused a delay. Many records are several excerpts of the same distress episode, so neither totals nor repeated language imply a count of distinct mothers.

### 43. Professional experience does not remove uncertainty with her own baby

A former nanny, nurses, teachers, medical providers and an occupational therapist explicitly report a gap between experience and being their own child's parent.

Count: 27 supporting quote records in this share.

Source-only: dv04-0233 identifies as a family-medicine doctor; dv04-0176 is restarting a business and studying for a degree; dv21-0174 studies part time at university. These specific disclosures do not establish a general income or education profile. The mother in dv06-0008 says she is enjoying motherhood despite practical constraints.

[dv21-0002]
> I’m definitely a thinker-type person, I can tend to get into my head a bit.  I feel like no one can 100% with confidence say YES or NO that there isn’t an issue.  I’m generally afraid that my kid is autistic and all the things I see with my pediatric patients at work is just going to be my life for the next 30 years.

[dv21-0054]
> I am convinced that the diagnosis is correct and I am absolutely spiraling. I KNOW that CP has a very wide range of severity. But as a mom, I can’t help but think the worst case. I KNOW that people with disabilities can live full and happy lives, but I can’t help but be scared that my son won’t be able to. I am terrified afraid he will be in pain. I am terrified he will never walk. I am terrified that he will have epilepsy or some severe intellectual disability, too. Being in the medical field, I see the worst. I see how adults with severe disabilities can end up in group homes and be poorly cared for after their parents die or can no longer care for them. I am scared that even if I’m able to take full and great care of him, that when I die he will be left to live in a home and be mistreated if he ends up not being able to care for himself due to severity of disability. I’m afraid that this stress is going to ruin my marriage. A selfish part of me is afraid that now I won’t be able to live the life I had planned for myself.

[dv21-0100]
> It just doesn’t feel that way. Between the slow milestones, and everything else, I just know this is something really bad. Im a special education teacher, and I’ve seen families ripped apart by their children with severe disabilities…siblings suffer, and I think out of the 50 to 60 families that I have worked with, I can think of maybe 4 to 5 that we’re still married, and it was always very wealthy families who to afford help. I have one family member with a disability that became evident around 18 months, so I assume a disability that’s evident from birth is something really really bad.

[dv21-0101]
> I did everything to prevent this. I had very low stakes for termination….like I would have terminated for any sort of major anomaly, even if it was compatible with life. The fact that I did IVF to prevent passing on a genetic disease only for him to have something worse is killing me. I obviously should not have had another kid if I didn’t consider how much having a severely disabled child would mess me up. I don’t know what to do now, but I’m highly considering adoption so another family can take better care of him, because I just don’t think I’m cut out for this as a parent.

[dv21-0102]
> It’s true, my special ed experience is clouding my judgment. I taught high school age students with very severe disabilities. I saw friends have kids, but wasn’t really there to witness milestones of a variety of typically developing kids.

### 44. Mental health difficulties are explicitly named by some

Some describe depression, PPD, anxiety, OCD, ADHD, medication, counselling, difficulty bonding or a loss of capacity; keep their descriptions specific.

Count: 101 supporting quote records in this share.

Only explicit self-reports establish named conditions. The partner's account in dv21-0152/0153 is second-hand and kept under other speakers below. dv21-0239 describes past thoughts of self-harm; dv21-0175 says she thinks the baby would be better off without her. These stay in full. Source-only: dv21-0011 takes Zoloft; dv04-0127 takes antidepressants and plans therapy; dv06-0065 reports PPD and postnatal OCD; dv21-0174 reports an OCD diagnosis since 17, is now 28, and discusses bipolar in a reply. No diagnosis is added by the reader.

[dv21-0008]
> I have PPD and have been having counselling and trying to get a bit more sleep which is helping. I am just really struggling with the bonding aspect. I would say I get a few seconds a day of my baby looking at my face and usually a smile with that but otherwise nothing. When I get a smile or sometimes a giggle from bouncing her around it feels incredible but it is so rare and short lived. Some days I feel fine about it but some days it's so hard and like we are getting very little back still. You can't really play with her either at this stage, it's just putting black and white cards in front of her still with little effect. I do just wonder how much more I would be enjoying motherhood if I felt I could interact with my child. There is of course also the ongoing concern about her general development which feels like a constant anxiety.

[dv21-0017]
> I just don’t know where to go for help I don’t want to go to the doctor and get prescribed medication as my mum did when she had pp depression and she is still taking meds now and has never really got the help she needed but that was back then.

[dv21-0116]
> I struggled a lot with ppd, have bpd and depression and anxiety, and have been in a depressive episode for about 6 months which I'm just coming out of. I'm an alcoholic who had 3 years sober but I started drinking in January and have only just started back in the rooms again which is saving my sanity. I've fucked up. I didnt interact enough with him while I was depressed. I played with him, sang, read, and all his needs were met, but obviously not enough. 
> 
> I'll admit there were times when I left him playing by himself on his mat and didn't interact with him a whole lot, but not for a long time or anything, and I never left him alone. I'm always talking to him, singing, smiling at him, even if I'm in a depressive slump and can't get myself off the sofa for an hour or so.
> 
>   
> I don't know what I'm even asking, but basically my baby is delayed and its all my fault and I dont know what to do right now to stop myself spiralling into a hole of self hatred and guilt.

[dv21-0152]
> Her specific worries jump around every day, and culminate in marathon googling and AI sessions, crying, and sleeping through the day out of sadness. But the main concern always boils down to a fear of a later autism diagnosis.

### 45. Physical health can limit the same activities she is told to do

Pain, postpartum recovery, pregnancy, fatigue and a baby's weight can make walking, carrying or floor play physically difficult.

The mother in dv21-0204 is 5'1 and carries a nearly 25lb nine-month-old. The marathon runner in dv06-0189 reports good physical endurance and mental exhaustion instead.

### 46. Love can coexist with resentment, boredom, regret and wanting to escape

Posters can love their child and also dislike the current stage, miss their life, dread the day, wish for work or another caregiver, or doubt becoming a parent.

A child being wanted does not prevent this: dv05-0125 source describes five years hoping for a baby; dv05-0234 three years trying and fear of a new pregnancy now. Do not collapse frustration, regret, a clinical condition and an intention to act into a single claim.

### 47. Fears of disability can involve the child's whole future and the family's future

Some fear exclusion, pain, lifelong care, marriage breakdown, losing their expected life or being unable to parent a disabled child; other parents explicitly reject panic about a possible diagnosis.

Count: 115 supporting quote records in this share.

The direct disclosures about abortion thresholds, IVF to avoid a genetic disease and considering adoption are retained in dv21-0101. dv21-0054 includes fear of life after the parents die and a threatened marriage. These are posters' fears and value statements; they do not establish a child's diagnosis or actual future. A father with clinical work is separated from mothers' first-person evidence below.

[dv21-0001]
> So my wife (I’m dad) took her to doctor appointment and said that my second daughter (24 months) is behind on talking.  According to my wife, our doctor referred her to speach therapy which we’re going for an assessment on Wednesday.  As far as I know, autism was NOT mentionted (I wasn’t there).  On a side note, my close friend just said his two year old was diagnosed as autistic very recently as well and now it’s on the brain.

[dv21-0007]
> To be honest, I’m going through grief right now.   I’ve accepted that an autism diagnosis is probably coming our way.  I’m prepared to change my life in any way to make sure his needs are taken care of.  My boy is so beautiful and perfect to me that sometimes I tear up when I look at him.  I’m just having a hard time processing that the world can be really ugly to kids who are different.  I think about my kid struggling to make friends, being excluded, and feeling like an outsider. ## Who is speaking who is not her

### 49. Fathers and other partners also describe their own parenting day

The records labelled partner include fathers with screen disagreements, work schedules, exhaustion, regret, clinical experience or a stay-at-home role; their accounts are separate evidence.

Count: 46 supporting quote records in this share.

There are 46 records carrying the partner label, not 46 fathers. The source behind dv06-0155 explicitly calls herself FTM and mentions her wife: preserve that first-time mother's self-description rather than turning the schema label into a male identity. dv21-0154 is a father who left a salaried job because daycare was expensive and his wife's job had more growth potential; he reports sexist treatment by an evaluator. Mother-account dv21-0175 says scrutiny falls on her rather than dad. Do not make either account universal.
